| STAB-0467 | ✅ | P3 | Verify glTF animation timestamps are in seconds | `mc3togltf/src/GltfExporter.cpp` | The row assumed an mc3-frame-units → glTF-seconds conversion; there isn't one, because there's no frame-rate concept in mc3 at all — `duration`/keyframe `time` are already in seconds, and `kf.time` passes straight through into the glTF sampler's `input` accessor with zero conversion applied (verified: no frame-rate/fps constant anywhere in `GltfExporter.cpp`). Added an explicit "Units" note to `MC3_FORMAT.md`'s Animations section documenting this — also fixed a stale line in the same file's export-support matrix (SVG textures were still documented as producing no warning at all, before this session's STAB-0440 fix). |
| STAB-0468 | ✅ | P3 | Verify animation with identical keyframe times (duplicate frames) | `mc3/src/Mc3XmlParser.cpp` | **Found a real gap and fixed it**: keyframes were sorted with plain `std::sort` (not stability-guaranteed for equal keys), so which of two identical-time keyframes ended up first was formally unspecified even though it happened to work in practice. Fixed: switched to `std::stable_sort`, giving a well-defined, documented policy — **first-declared keyframe wins** the tie (preserves XML document order). Also confirmed no crash/NaN risk in `evaluateChannel()`: Linear interpolation explicitly guards a zero-length time span (`if (span <= 0.0f) return k0.value;`), and CubicBezier uses a bisection search (`solveBezierU`, no direct division), so it's inherently immune to a zero-span segment. Added `testDuplicateKeyframeTimePolicy()` (8 assertions) confirming both the tie-break order and finite (non-NaN) evaluation at and around the duplicate time. |
| STAB-0469 | ✅ | P3 | Verify animation merge: merge scene adds actions without clobbering existing | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication_FileOps.cpp` | **Found a real gap and fixed it**: `mergeSceneFromFile()` merged textures (skip-on-collision), materials (suffix-on-collision), and objects (always appended) — but never touched `src.actions` at all, so merging a scene silently discarded 100% of its animations. Fixed by adding the same suffix-on-collision merge pattern already used for materials; no channel-reference remapping needed since the merged objects keep their original names unchanged. Verified by code reading + a clean rebuild — `mergeSceneFromFile()` is only reachable from a live UI popup dialog (`MeshCraftApplication_UiOverlays.cpp`), not extracted into a headlessly-testable Alg mirror, so this wasn't exercised by an automated test (same verification depth as STAB-0434). **Separately noted, not fixed**: the *texture* merge in the same function still uses skip-on-collision (existing wins) rather than the suffix+reference-remap pattern STAB-0425 established for `ModelRegistry::insertIntoScene` — a merged material could end up referencing the wrong texture if names collide with different values. Flagged as a related, out-of-scope-for-this-task finding (see `NEXT.md` §5). |
| STAB-0470 | ✅ | P3 | Add animation XSD roundtrip: `interpolation` attribute preserved | `mc3/test/roundtrip_test.cpp` | Already covered: `testAnimationStep()` round-trips a 3-keyframe channel with `interp="step"` and asserts `Interpolation::Step` survives exactly for each keyframe. `testAnimationCubicBezier()` similarly covers `interp="cubic"` with handle data, and `testAnimationLinear`/most other tests cover the default `linear`. All 3 interpolation modes have real roundtrip coverage; no new test needed. |

Added `testConvertToDefinition()` (20 assertions): confirms the definition entry is a deep copy with transform reset to identity (position/rotation/scale) but original type/children preserved; the replacement Instance preserves src's original transform/visibility/tags/id/name and correctly references the new definition key; the object count stays the same (in-place replacement, not appended); and a second conversion gets a distinct key (`def_2`, no collision with `def_1`). |
| STAB-0483 | ✅ | P2 | Verify "Break Instance" expands to copy of definition content | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication_Commands.cpp` | **Found a real bug and fixed it**: the original code only uniquified the *top-level* copy's id (`copy->id = inst->id;` was set then immediately overwritten by an unused dead assignment — the real uniquification loop generated an id from `copy->name`) — every **descendant** kept the definition template's original id verbatim. Breaking a second Instance of the *same* definition (a common workflow, e.g. placing multiple copies of a prop) produced exact duplicate child ids, which matters since `id` is used elsewhere as a set key (`lockedIds_`, 43 references). Extracted `breakInstanceAlg()` + a new `regenerateSubtreeIdsAlg()` helper (recursively assigns a fresh, document-unique id to the copy and every descendant) + `flatFindByIdAlg()` (mirror of `flatFindById`) into `EditorAlgorithms.hpp`; wired the real `breakInstance()` to call it. Added `testBreakInstance()` (20 assertions): confirms the copy preserves the instance's name/transform/visibility/tags but takes the definition's type/children, rejects non-Instance objects and Instances with an unknown definition, and — the actual bug — breaking two Instances of the same definition produces **zero** duplicate ids anywhere in the resulting document (verified by walking every id in both subtrees into a set and checking each insert succeeds). |

Wired the real `alignToObject()` to call it; as a side effect, fixed the status-message count to reflect objects *actually* aligned (previously it always reported `selectionSize-1`, over-counting if any selected object was locked and skipped). Added `testAlignToObject()` (11 assertions) covering the align itself, rotation being left untouched, locked-object skip, and the <2-selected/empty no-op cases. |

No Alg mirror existed for this command (same gap as STAB-0482/0483/0484). Extracted `ScatterCurveParamsAlg` + `scatterCurvePositionAlg()` (pure line/arc position math) + `scatterAlongCurveAlg()` (main splice-and-copy loop, taking an injectable `jitterRng` for determinism) into `EditorAlgorithms.hpp`; wired the real `scatterAlongCurve()` to build params from the existing UI members and call it, keeping the wall-clock-seeded jitter lambda as the injected RNG. Added `testScatterAlongCurve()` (21 assertions): confirms count=10/selected=1 -> exactly 9 new objects and the original is untouched; multiple selected sources each independently get `count-1` copies inserted right after themselves in their own parent list; line-mode and arc-mode position math match `scatterCurvePositionAlg`; a fixed non-zero jitter function produces exact, deterministic per-axis offsets; and `count<2`/empty-selection are no-ops. |
| STAB-0486 | ✅ | P2 | Verify command palette executes same code path as menu | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication_UiOverlays.cpp` | Most palette entries already call the exact same shared function as their Edit-menu equivalent (`deleteSelected()`, `duplicateSelected()`, `groupSelected()`, etc.) — correct. **Found 2 real divergences**: (1) Undo/Redo had 3 independent hand-copied implementations — keyboard shortcut (`MeshCraftApplication_Keyboard.cpp`), Edit menu, and command palette — that had silently drifted: only the keyboard path enforced the `kUndoMax` cap on the *opposite* stack (redo-stack growth on Undo, undo-stack growth on Redo) and called `evaluateAndPushAnimOverrides()` to refresh animation state; undoing/redoing via the menu or command palette could grow the opposite stack past the 20-entry cap and left stale animation overrides applied to the new document state until something else (e.g. playback) refreshed it. (2) "Drop to Ground Plane" was duplicated in the menu and palette with a cosmetic status-message drift (menu reported the actual dropped count, palette always said "Dropped to ground plane" with no count). Fixed both by extracting shared `performUndo()`/`performRedo()`/`dropSelectedToGroundPlane()` private methods (`MeshCraftApplication_Commands.cpp`) and wiring all 3 (keyboard/menu/palette) — or 2, for the ground-plane case — call sites to them, eliminating the duplication so future changes can't re-diverge. No new headless test possible (these are CNA-coupled `MeshCraftApplication` methods, can't be instantiated in `mc3_commands_test` — same constraint as every other real-editor-method fix this session); the shared push-with-cap pattern itself is already covered by `testUndoStackDepthCapped`/`pushWithCapAlg`. Verified via full rebuild (0 warnings) + 25/25 ctest + a real `--screenshot` smoke test against `house.mc3.xml`. |
| STAB-0487 | ✅ | P2 | Verify macro recorder captures batchRename step | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication_Macro.cpp` | Confirmed correct, already covered: `batchRenameSelected()` (`MeshCraftApplication_Commands.cpp:443`) unconditionally calls `recordStep("batch_rename", {batchRenameBuf_})` after applying the rename — `recordStep()` itself early-returns unless `isRecording_` is true, so it's a no-op outside a recording session. Playback (`executeMacroStep`) correctly reverses this: `copyToBuf(batchRenameBuf_, arg(0)); batchRenameSelected();`, replaying through the exact same real function (not a parallel implementation). The save/load side (`.mc3macro` tab-separated format) is already exhaustively tested by `testMacroSaveLoadRoundTrip()` (STAB-0292), whose very first fixture includes a `"batch_rename"` step with its pattern arg, confirming it survives a save→load round-trip byte-for-byte. No bug found, no code change needed. |
| STAB-0488 | ✅ | P2 | Verify macro playback: step for nonexistent object name is skipped | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication_Macro.cpp` | Row's phrasing assumes a name-lookup-and-skip mechanism that doesn't exist by design: `MacroStep`/`MacroStepAlg` never store an object name or id at all — every recorded verb (`add`/`delete`/`duplicate`/`group`/`ungroup`/`group_scale`/`batch_rename`/`linear_array`/`hide`/`show_all`/`lock`/`unlock`) operates on whatever is in `selection_` *at playback time*, not on a recorded reference to a specific object. Verified every selection-consuming command has an explicit empty-selection guard (`duplicateSelected()`, `groupSelected()`, `ungroupSelected()`, `groupScaleSelected()`, `arrayDuplicate()`, `batchRenameSelected()` — all `if (!hasSelection()) return;` or equivalent at the top), so replaying a macro recorded on "Cube" in a scene where nothing (or something else) is selected at playback time is already a safe no-op for every step — not a crash, and not a wrong-object mutation. This is the correct architecture (selection-relative macros, not name-bound scripts) — no bug, no code change needed. |
| STAB-0489 | ✅ | P2 | Verify proportional editing: nearby objects influenced | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication_Mouse.cpp` | Read the `applyFalloff` lambda inside `handleMouseInput()` — the Gaussian falloff math (weight = 1.0 at zero distance, decreasing to 0 at the radius edge exclusive, skipping selected and locked objects, centered on the average of the selection's positions) was already correct, but entirely untested (the whole function is a single giant mouse-drag handler, no Alg mirror existed for this piece). Extracted `proportionalFalloffWeightAlg()` (pure weight formula) + `applyProportionalFalloffAlg()` (selection-center + recursive walk + apply, taking an already-resolved world-space delta since axis resolution is mouse/gizmo-driven and out of scope for a CNA-free mirror) into `EditorAlgorithms.hpp`; wired the real code to call it (removed ~25 duplicated lines). Added `testProportionalFalloff()` (14 assertions): the weight function is 1.0 at zero distance, exactly 0 at and beyond the radius edge, strictly decreasing in between, and 0 for a non-positive radius; the apply function only nudges unselected+unlocked objects within radius (confirmed via a near/far/locked-neighbor scene), correctly uses the *average* of a multi-object selection as the influence center, and is a no-op for an empty selection or a non-positive radius. Verified via full rebuild (0 warnings), 25/25 ctest, and a real `--screenshot` smoke test against `house.mc3.xml`. |

No Alg mirror existed. Extracted `vertexSnapToNearestAlg()` into `EditorAlgorithms.hpp` (search + rigid-offset apply, taking an already-resolved reference point since the anchor-object and threshold derivation are mouse/camera-driven); wired the real code to call it (removed ~25 duplicated lines). Added `testVertexSnap()` (11 assertions): a found target snaps the anchor exactly onto it and moves every other selected object by the identical offset (rigid, not independently re-centered); no target within threshold is a no-op returning `false`; the *nearest* of several candidates wins, not just the first found; a locked selected object is excluded from the actual move even though the target search itself doesn't consult lock state; empty selection is a no-op. The rounding itself (`std::round(r / snapRotate_) * snapRotate_`) is correct and generic for any increment. Also confirmed the Ctrl-held momentary override is **intentionally rotation-only**: Move/Scale drags only check the persistent `snapEnabled_` toggle, while rotation checks `snapEnabled_ || ctrlHeld` — verified this asymmetry is deliberate, not a bug, via the matching status-bar snap indicator in `MeshCraftApplication_UiOverlays.cpp` which uses the identical "Ctrl or snap grid" condition. No Alg mirror existed for the rotate-drag loop. Added `testRotationDragSnap()` (12 assertions): delta accumulates on only the targeted axis across multiple objects; locked objects are skipped and excluded from the count; a post-delta value snaps correctly both up and down to the nearest 45° increment; empty selection is a no-op. Verified via full rebuild (0 warnings), 25/25 ctest, and a real `--screenshot` smoke test against `house.mc3.xml`. |

No Alg mirror existed. Extracted `flattenDescendantsAlg()` into `EditorAlgorithms.hpp`; wired the real function to call it (removed ~8 duplicated lines). Added `testFlattenDescendants()` (5 assertions) with a 3-level tree (children → grandchildren → one great-grandchild): confirms all 5 descendants are present regardless of depth, pre-order (parent before its own children/grandchildren), and a childless object flattens to an empty list. Verified via full rebuild (0 warnings), 25/25 ctest, and a real `--screenshot` smoke test against `house.mc3.xml`. |
| STAB-0493 | ✅ | P2 | Verify "Random variant" creates instance pointing to random definition | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication_Commands.cpp` | Row's file is wrong — there is no editor command for this; it's a data-model feature (`Mc3Object::variantDefinitions` + `makeInstanceVariant()`, `mc3/src/Mc3Object.cpp`), resolved deterministically per-object by hashing the object's own `id` (not a true per-call random pick — stable across runs, which is the correct design for reproducible exports). **Found a real bug and fixed it**: `SceneRenderer.cpp` (live viewport) and `CsgEvaluator.cpp` (glTF CSG evaluation) both already resolved variants correctly via the identical hash formula (duplicated in each), but `GltfExporter.cpp`'s regular (non-CSG) Instance path ignored `variantDefinitions` entirely and always used `definition` (i.e. always the *first* variant) — an Instance with variants used as a normal scene node rendered correctly in the editor viewport but always exported as its first variant regardless of id. Its mesh-cache key had the same bug compounded: keyed off `obj.definition` (always the same value for every variant instance of a given definition list) rather than the resolved key, which would have caused *wrong* mesh reuse across differently-resolved variant instances once the primary bug was fixed, had the cache key not been fixed at the same time. Also found and fixed the same gap in `breakInstanceAlg()` (STAB-0483, this session) and in `SceneRenderer_Extrude.cpp`'s edge-overlay wireframe path (would have drawn the wrong variant's outline vs. its own solid mesh), plus a definition-rename gap in the left panel's "rename definition key" flow that patched `Instance::definition` but not matching entries inside `variantDefinitions` (a renamed definition that was one of several variants would leave a dangling reference). Consolidated all of this into one new shared, non-breaking `Mc3Object::resolvedInstanceDefinitionKey()` method (`mc3/include/MeshCraft/Mc3/Mc3Object.hpp` + `.cpp`) and wired every consumer (`SceneRenderer.cpp`, `CsgEvaluator.cpp`, `GltfExporter.cpp`, `breakInstanceAlg`, the rename-fixup loop) through it — single source of truth, no more drift possible. Added `test/instance_variant.mc3.xml` (8 instances, distinct ids, same 2-entry variant list) + `mc3togltf_instance_variant` ctest: asserts real variation occurs across the 8 instances (exactly 2 distinct meshes used, not a collapse onto one — this is the regression check that would have failed pre-fix) and that resolution is deterministic across repeated export runs. Verified via full CMake reconfigure + rebuild (0 warnings), 26/26 ctest (up from 25/25), a real `--screenshot` smoke test against `house.mc3.xml`, and a standalone (CNA-free) `mc3togltf` re-verification. |
| STAB-0494 | ✅ | P3 | Add command test: arrayDuplicate with negative count fails gracefully | `mc3/test/editor_commands_test.cpp` | Row's "no objects added" expectation doesn't match `arrayDuplicateObjects()`'s actual (also graceful) design: `count = std::max(2, count);` at the top clamps any too-small count (0, 1, or negative) to the same minimum of 2, so exactly 1 copy is still created — never a silent zero-op, and never a crash. The real ImGui slider is clamped to `[2,20]` so a negative count can only be reached via a hand-edited/hostile `.mc3macro` `linear_array` step (`std::stoi` on untrusted file content) bypassing that clamp — the real robustness question this row is getting at. Added 2 new assertions to `testArrayDuplicate()`: `count=-1` produces exactly 1 copy (root grows by exactly 1, not 0 and not a runaway loop), and `count=-1000000` (guards against an extreme negative being misinterpreted as a huge unsigned loop count and hanging/OOMing) also safely produces exactly 1 copy. |
| STAB-0495 | ✅ | P3 | Verify Group Scale: positions scaled from selection centroid | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication_Commands.cpp` | Confirmed exactly correct against the row's own example: 2 objects at (0,0,0) and (2,0,0), group-scaled 2x, end up at (-1,0,0) and (3,0,0) (centroid 1,0,0, each pushed twice as far from it). No Alg mirror existed. Extracted `groupScaleAlg()` into `EditorAlgorithms.hpp` (centroid computation + per-object position-from-centroid + own-scale multiply, skip locked, return scaled count); wired the real `groupScaleSelected()` to call it (removed ~20 duplicated lines), and as a side effect fixed its status-message count to reflect objects *actually* scaled (previously always reported the full selection size, over-counting if any selected object was locked — same fix shape as STAB-0484's `alignToObject`). Added `testGroupScale()` (12 assertions): the row's own 2x-scale example verified exactly; factor<1 shrinks toward the centroid; a locked object is excluded from the move/scale and the count, but still correctly contributes to the centroid average; empty selection is a no-op. Verified via full rebuild (0 warnings), 26/26 ctest, and a real `--screenshot` smoke test against `house.mc3.xml`. **This closes S13 (Commands, Undo/Redo, and Algorithms) entirely.** |

Added `test/background_texture.mc3.xml` (a solid bright-green `background_texture`, generated as a committed `test/textures/solid_green.png` asset, behind a bright red foreground box) + `test/background_texture_test.py` (real pixel sampling: asserts both a substantial green cluster — the background showing through where the box doesn't cover it — and a substantial red cluster — the box rendering on top of it, proving correct draw order) + a permanent `background_texture_test` ctest. | STAB-0524 | ✅ | P3 | Verify skybox panorama shader: equirectangular image displayed | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication.cpp` | **Found and fixed a real, previously-invisible bug**: the skybox never rendered anything, despite `document_.environment->skyboxTexture` being read correctly, the shader compiling and linking (valid program ID), the texture loading successfully, and every GL state check (viewport, scissor, depth/blend/cull-face, current program/framebuffer, `glGetError()`) coming back exactly as expected — confirmed even a hardcoded-solid-color fragment shader produced zero visible pixels. Root cause: `drawSkybox()` built and bound a dedicated `quadVAO`/`quadVBO` with a `layout(location=0) in vec2 a_pos` vertex attribute, but that VAO-based path silently produces a degenerate/invisible draw in this environment (OpenGL ES 3.2 via Mesa) — while the bloom passes' `gl_VertexID`-based procedural full-screen-quad generation (no VAO/vertex-attribute needed) is confirmed working. Fixed by switching `kSkyboxVS` to the same `gl_VertexID` pattern, binding VAO 0 instead of `gl.quadVAO`, and removing the now-dead VAO/VBO construction from `initSkybox()`. Also fixed a related bug found in the same function: `drawSkybox()` was always passed the default editor camera's `fovDegrees` (60°) instead of the actual active camera's FOV when look-through-camera mode is active — added an `effectiveFovDegrees` local that tracks the real active FOV (walk-mode/look-through-camera override), matching the `view`/`proj` matrices already computed for those modes. Added `test/skybox_texture.mc3.xml` (a solid-blue equirectangular `skybox_texture`, no background/foreground) + a new committed test asset `test/textures/solid_blue_equirect.png` + `test/skybox_texture_test.py` (real pixel sampling within a safe viewport-interior rectangle: asserts >80% blue-dominant coverage) + a permanent `skybox_texture_test` ctest. Manually confirmed 96% blue coverage (10,353/10,752 samples) after the fix, vs. 0% before. Verified via full CMake reconfigure + rebuild (0 warnings from MeshCraft's own sources) and 38/38 ctest (up from 37/37). |
| STAB-0525 | ✅ | P3 | Verify LOD: primitive segment count decreases at distance | `src/MeshCraft/Renderer/SceneRenderer.cpp` | Confirmed the LOD tier selection (`SceneRenderer.cpp:633-639`, G8) is camera-distance-driven, not a UI toggle: squared distance from `camPosX_/Y_/Z_` (extracted from the view matrix) to the object's world pivot picks tier 0 (<10 units, full 32-segment mesh), 1 (10–40, 16-segment), or 2 (>40, 6-segment) for Sphere/Cylinder/Cone/Torus/Capsule via the `lodMesh()` helper. No accessor existed to observe which tier was actually used, so added `lodLevelMap_`/`lastLodLevel(objId)` (same accessor pattern as `csgCachedTriCount()`/K4 and `csgCacheEvaluationCount()`/STAB-0522), populated every draw, printed as `[LOD] level=N` for the scene's first object at the end of `--screenshot` mode. Added `test/lod_near.mc3.xml` (a sphere 3 units from the camera, expect tier 0) + `test/lod_far.mc3.xml` (the identical sphere 60 units away, expect tier 2) + `test/lod_test.py` (runs both scenes, asserts the reported tier is exactly 0 near / 2 far, not just that some level is printed) + a permanent `lod_test` ctest. Manually confirmed level 0 near / level 2 far. This closes out S14 (Rendering and Viewport Stability) — 17 done, 13 flagged 🟡, 0 remaining. Verified via full CMake reconfigure + rebuild (0 warnings from MeshCraft's own sources) and 39/39 ctest (up from 38/38). |

Added `mc3togltf/test/include_export_test.py` (parses the GLB, confirms each Instance node of an included definition has real, non-empty mesh geometry, and that the two identical-definition instances share one mesh) + a permanent `mc3togltf_include_export` ctest. |
| STAB-0549 | ✅ | P3 | Verify embedded assets during GLB export: `embed:` meshSource resolved | `mc3togltf/src/GltfExporter.cpp` | **Confirmed as a documented, deliberate limitation, not a bug to fix** (matching the row's own anticipated resolution, "marked as limitation"): `buildMesh()` (`:417-424`) treats `obj.meshSource` unconditionally as a literal OBJ file path — no special-case for the `"embed:<id>"` form `Mc3XmlParser`/`Writer` already parse/serialize/round-trip correctly. Actually resolving an embed reference would mean parsing the referenced external `.glb` (or decoding inline base64 GLB data) and merging its meshes into the exported model — a genuine new feature, not a quick fix, and not attempted here (same judgment as STAB-0519/0526's precedent for scope decisions requiring a real feature). Manually confirmed the degradation is safe: exit 0, one `Warning: OBJ load failed (.../embed:tree): Cannot open file`, and a valid GLB with an empty (meshless) node for the affected object — matching `NEXT.md`'s existing "What does NOT work yet" documentation exactly. Added `test/embed_mesh_source.mc3.xml` + `mc3togltf/test/embed_mesh_source_test.py` (locks in this exact graceful-degradation behavior — exit 0, warning present, empty node — so a future change to `buildMesh()` can't silently regress this into a crash without a test noticing) + a permanent `mc3togltf_embed_mesh_source` ctest. Verified via full CMake reconfigure + rebuild (0 warnings from `mc3togltf`'s own sources) and 46/46 ctest (up from 44/44). |
| STAB-0550 | ✅ | P3 | Verify subtree template import resolves relative paths | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication_Commands.cpp` | **Found and fixed a real, deeper bug — the 4th instance of this session's "reference silently breaks across a merge/import boundary" pattern, but this time in the core `<include>` mechanism itself, not just one export dialog**: there is no dedicated "import subtree template" function — a template exported by `exportSubtreeAsTemplate()` (STAB-0536) is brought back in via the same `<include>` mechanism `mc3_library.mc3.xml` uses. `Mc3XmlParser.cpp`'s `mergeInclude()` (called for every `<include>`) stores an included file's `<texture uri="...">` and any `<mesh src="...">` inside an included `<definition>` **completely unadjusted** — but everything in a document resolves against a single `doc.sourcePath` (the *main* file's own directory, set once at the top of `Mc3XmlParser::parse()`). If the included/template file lives in a *different* directory than the main scene (its whole reason for existing — a shared, reusable library), any relative path inside it silently resolves against the wrong directory once merged in. This went unnoticed because both existing include fixtures (`scene_with_include.mc3.xml`, `mc3_library.mc3.xml`) happen to share the same directory by coincidence. Fixed by adding `rebaseRelativePath()` (converts a path relative to the included file's own directory into one relative to `doc.sourcePath` instead) and `rebaseDefinitionMeshSources()` (recursively applies it to every `Mesh`-type object inside an included definition's subtree, skipping `embed:` references), wired into `mergeInclude()` for textures (including external SVG textures' `src`), and included-definition mesh sources. Since the writer already skips re-emitting included content entirely (confirmed by STAB-0540's own test), this in-memory-only rebase never leaks into a saved file and is recomputed fresh (correctly) on every load. Added `testIncludeAcrossDirectoriesRebasesRelativePaths` (`mc3/test/roundtrip_test.cpp`) — builds a real two-directory fixture (`assets/lib.mc3.xml` + `scenes/scene.mc3.xml` `<include>`ing it via a relative `../` path) with a real texture file and confirms the rebased URI/meshSource both resolve to the actual files on disk, not just a plausible-looking string. Pure `mc3`-lib fix and test, CNA-free. Verified via full rebuild and 46/46 ctest (same count, more assertions inside the existing `mc3_roundtrip` ctest; also reconfirmed the pre-existing same-directory `testInclude` still passes unchanged). |

This is a CNA-side backend/graphics-API configuration decision, out of scope to fix here per this repo's CNA boundary — needs the CNA maintainer to either vendor GLES headers for Windows or select a desktop-GL code path for non-Linux targets. (Converting an actual file via Node hit a `"file not found"` — expected, since this build target has no `NODERAWFS`/`--preload-file` host-filesystem wiring, which is a browser-canvas deployment concern outside this row's own "`.html` loads in browser" ask, not a bug.) `b-web/` (286 MB scratch build dir) removed after verification — this environment has no browser to literally confirm page load, so that specific half-sentence remains for a human, but every part reachable from a CLI is fully green. **Update — actually tested in a real browser (user-driven, served via a local `python3 -m http.server`)**: the page loads, `SDL_CreateWindow` succeeds, `EasyGLGraphicsBackend` initializes over **WebGL 2.0** (`OpenGL ES 3.0 (WebGL 2.0)`), and `[MeshCraft] New scene` prints — no crash, no console error — but the canvas renders as a blank/black rectangle; the 3D editor UI is not visually usable. The actual reason nothing visible renders (ImGui font-atlas upload failing under WebGL2? some GL call unsupported and silently no-oping? something else per-frame) was **not** root-caused — doing so would need temporary MeshCraft-side frame-counter/diagnostic logging plus another rebuild-and-user-retest round-trip, which the user opted to defer. **Net effect on this row: still ✅** — "`emcmake cmake` succeeds" and "loads in browser" (in the sense of: loads, initializes, no crash) are both genuinely true. Since `meshcraftConfigDir()` (`MeshCraftPrivate.hpp`, fixed for Windows in STAB-0558) resolves to `$HOME/.config/meshcraft` on non-Windows targets including Emscripten, every file MeshCraft writes automatically (`prefs.ini`, `recent.txt`, `keybindings.ini`, `macro.mc3macro`, and any scene saved under the default `$HOME`) was landing in a plain in-memory MEMFS directory, not the IDBFS-mounted one — it would have silently failed to survive a page reload despite `pre.js`'s sync callbacks reporting no error (they were syncing the *wrong* directory). Fixed by mounting IDBFS at `/home/web_user` instead. Rebuilt the full Emscripten target (449/449, exit 0) and confirmed the compiled `MeshCraft.js` now references `/home/web_user` (was `/home/user`). | STAB-0566 | ✅ | P3 | Verify Windows: SDL runtime DLLs copied | `CMakeLists.txt` | **Initially closed on code-review alone (incorrectly) — actually reconfiguring against the MinGW toolchain (reusing STAB-0552's cached `.sdl-prebuilt-Windows-x86_64`, no need for a full build) found and fixed a real bug.** `CMakeLists.txt:345-347` calls `cna_copy_sdl_runtime(${_target})`, whose definition (`cna/cmake/ThirdPartySDL.cmake:265-278`) copies a DLL for each of `SDL3::SDL3`/`SDL3_image::SDL3_image`/`SDL3_mixer::SDL3_mixer` *if that CMake target is visible* in the calling scope. `cmake --trace-expand` against a real MinGW configure showed all three `if(TARGET ...)` checks evaluating **false** at MeshCraft's call site, and the resulting `build.ninja` had zero SDL DLL copy commands (only `libwinpthread-1.dll`, from the separate `cna_copy_mingw_runtime`) — because those targets are `find_package(SDL3 CONFIG)`-created `IMPORTED`/`ALIAS` targets local to CNA's own `add_subdirectory()` scope, invisible to MeshCraft's parent-level `CMakeLists.txt` (an IMPORTED-target directory-scoping subtlety, not something wrong with `cna_copy_sdl_runtime` itself in its own intended call site — CNA's own demos call it from the same scope where the targets were created, so it works fine there). **Fixed entirely on the MeshCraft side** (no CNA changes, per the CNA boundary): added a fallback right after the existing call, in `CMakeLists.txt`'s `if(WIN32)` block, that copies `SDL3.dll`/`SDL3_image.dll`/`SDL3_mixer.dll` directly via the `CNA_SDL_PREBUILT_ROOT` CACHE variable (globally visible, unlike the IMPORTED targets), guarded by `if(EXISTS ...)` so it's a no-op when using `CNA_USE_SYSTEM_SDL` or on non-Windows. Verified: reconfigured against the MinGW toolchain and confirmed `build.ninja`'s `MeshCraft.exe` `POST_BUILD` now includes all 3 SDL DLL copies plus the pre-existing pthread DLL copy; Linux Debug rebuild unaffected, 46/46 `ctest` still pass. Scratch `b-mingw/` (186 MB) removed after. Full build still can't complete end-to-end here (STAB-0552's CNA-side GLES3 blocker), so the *actual* runtime file-copy was verified via the generated build graph, not an executed link. |

The actual "property accessors" the verification text means live in `PropertiesPanel.cpp` (audited instead, since that's where the real risk would be): the entire ~1760-line property-editing body is gated by a single `if (ctx.selection.hasSelection())` (line 36–1796), so `sel0` is never dereferenced on an empty selection; every `std::optional` field access (`primitive`/`extrude`/`deform`/`uv_mapping`) is guarded by `has_value()`/truthiness immediately before `*`/`.value()`; both `std::map::find()` results (`definitions`, `materials`) are checked against `.end()` before dereferencing `->second`. No unguarded null/empty access found. |
| STAB-0601 | ✅ | P1 | Audit `SceneRenderer.cpp` for null-pointer risk | `src/MeshCraft/Renderer/SceneRenderer.cpp` | Checked every `.find()`/`->second`/`.value()`/`.at()` call site (grep-driven, then read each in context) plus the two possibly-null-returning cache helpers (`loadOrGetTexture`/`loadOrGetMesh`, which can return `nullptr` on a load failure) and both of their call sites. Every one is properly guarded: `doc.materials.find(...)`/`doc.textures.find(...)`/`doc.definitions.find(...)` all checked against `.end()` before use; `obj.primitive.value()`/`obj.extrude.value()` both guarded by an adjacent truthiness check; `csgMeshCache_`'s find-or-insert-then-refind pattern guarantees a valid iterator before dereferencing; `loadOrGetTexture`'s result is passed into `drawMeshTextured`, which itself null-checks `tex` before using it; `loadOrGetMesh`'s result is checked with `if (loaded)` before dereferencing. No unguarded null-pointer risk found. |

| STAB-0604 | ✅ | P2 | Audit duplicate code: `SceneRenderer_Builders.cpp` vs `MeshBuilder.cpp` | both files | Read both in full (box + UV sphere compared line-by-line as representative samples). Same underlying parametric math (identical ring/sector UV-sphere formula, same 6-face box topology) but genuinely different code shapes, not extractable without a conversion-layer abstraction: `SceneRenderer_Builders.cpp` builds unit-sized (0.5 half-extent) shapes directly into 2 different interleaved GPU vertex formats (`VertexPositionColor` + `VertexPositionNormalTexture`, the latter unshared-per-face for flat shading) via `uint16_t`-indexed `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer` uploads (CNA-coupled, `device_`-dependent); `MeshBuilder.cpp` builds arbitrarily-dimensioned shapes into flat `positions`/`normals`/`texcoords`/`uint32_t indices` vectors for `tinygltf` accessors (CNA-free), with a shared `addQuad()` helper and axis-remapping not present on the renderer side. Extraction would add an abstraction layer solely to bridge these representations, with no runtime benefit and real risk to two independently-tested core paths (live viewport, glTF export) — not done. |
| STAB-0605 | ✅ | P2 | Verify logging is consistent (no mix of printf/cerr/custom logger) | all `.cpp` | Grep-driven audit across `mc3/src`, `mc3togltf/src`, `mcb/src`, `mc3tomcb/src`, `src/MeshCraft`: 0 raw `printf`/`fprintf` calls anywhere (the only `printf`-family hits are `std::snprintf` formatting into local string buffers — UI labels/XML number formatting, not diagnostics, a different concern). Libraries (`mc3/`, `mc3togltf/`, `mcb/`) consistently use `std::cerr` with a `"Warning: "`/`"Error: "` prefix and never touch stdout; `mcb/src` has zero output at all. CLI tools (`mc3togltf`, `mc3tomcb`) use `std::cout` for their actual output plus `std::cerr`/`"Error: "` for failures — appropriate, since a CLI's stdout *is* its product. The editor (`src/MeshCraft/`) tags every message with a `[Subsystem]` prefix (`[MeshCraft]`, `[Bloom]`, `[Skybox]`, `[SSAO]`, `[ShadowDebug]`, `[Screenshot]`) to `std::cout` (info) or `std::cerr` (error) — consistent within itself, and appropriate for a desktop app with no other UI channel for startup/runtime diagnostics. No custom logger exists, and none is needed given this consistency. |

The row itself hedges this as "Optional." Adding a parallel non-throwing `loadFromFile(path, &error)` variant would be speculative API surface with zero current consumer — not done, per the project's "no features beyond what's required" policy. |

Object ownership uses `std::make_shared<Mc3::Mc3Object>()` (`McbReader.cpp:315`); everything else is `std::vector`/`std::string` RAII containers. |

All paths are `std::filesystem::path` (include-cycle detection uses `weakly_canonical`/`absolute`); actual disk I/O goes through tinyxml2's `LoadFile`/`SaveFile` (path-string based), not manual `FILE*` handling in our code. |
| STAB-0609 | ✅ | P2 | Investigate header-include hygiene (IWYU) | all headers | Ran `iwyu_tool` (8.23-1) against all 9 `mc3/src/*.cpp` files + their headers via the standalone `mc3/` build's `compile_commands.json`. Fixed 3 genuine, safe findings: removed truly-unused `#include <cmath>` (`Mc3Animation.cpp`) and `#include <cstdlib>` (`Mc3XmlParser.cpp`); changed `Mc3XmlWriter.hpp` to forward-declare `class Mc3Document` instead of including the full (large, aggregator) `Mc3Document.hpp`, adding the now-needed direct include to `Mc3XmlWriter.cpp` instead — reduces header coupling for anything that only includes the writer's own header. Left 2 categories of findings alone: (1) IWYU's suggestion to drop `#include <functional>` from `Mc3Object.cpp` despite it calling `std::hash<std::string>{}` — a false positive, since `<functional>` is `std::hash`'s canonical standard header regardless of what any specific stdlib implementation transitively provides; (2) the large "should add N transitive headers" lists for `Mc3XmlParser.cpp`/`Mc3XmlWriter.cpp` (each Mc3 sub-type header individually) — these rely on the existing, intentional "aggregator header" pattern (`Mc3Object.hpp`/`Mc3Document.hpp` pulling in the full type family) rather than a genuine bug, and mechanically adding 15-20 include lines per file across the parser/writer is exactly the kind of "mass refactoring of passing code" the stabilization phase's own guidance warns against for a lint-hygiene task. Verified: standalone `mc3/` build and the full Debug build both rebuild clean, 46/46 `ctest` still pass. |

Not split further. |
| STAB-0619 | ✅ | P3 | Verify MCB tag constants extracted to shared header | `mcb/src/` | Confirmed — see STAB-0147, done under the name `McbFormat.hpp` rather than the row's assumed `McbTags.hpp`. |
| STAB-0620 | ✅ | P3 | Investigate static analysis: cppcheck on Mc3 library | `mc3/src/*.cpp` | Ran `cppcheck --enable=all --std=c++23 -I mc3/include -I include mc3/src/` (2.17.1). **Zero `error`- or `warning`-severity findings** — only `style`/`performance`/`information`. 98 of the 101 non-informational findings are `unusedFunction` on `Mc3Object`'s fluent builder methods (`makeBox`/`makeSphere`/`withId`/etc.) — false positives from analyzing `mc3/src/` in isolation; these are real public builder-API methods used from `mc3togltf`, `mc3tomcb`, and the editor, just outside cppcheck's single-directory view. The remaining 3 are `passedByValue` perf suggestions (`Mc3Texture::withWrap(std::string u, std::string v)`, `Mc3Document::defineObject(std::string id, ...)`) — genuine micro-optimizations, but both are public API signatures; left as-is per `CLAUDE.md`'s "no `Mc3Document` public API changes without checking `mc3togltf`/`mc3tomcb`/test XMLs" boundary and the stabilization phase's "no mass refactoring of passing code" guidance — not worth the API-churn risk for a `const&` micro-opt. |

## S19 — Security and Robustness

| ID | St | Pri | Title | Key File(s) | Verification |
|----|----|-----|-------|-------------|--------------|
| STAB-0621 | ✅ | P0 | Verify API key not stored in any file | `src/MeshCraft/AiAssistant.cpp`, prefs.ini | `grep -rn apiKey` across every persisted-state writer (`EditorAlgorithms.hpp`'s `savePrefsAlg`/`saveMacroAlg`/keybindings, `MeshCraftApplication_FileOps.cpp`'s autosave/recent-files) returns 0 hits — the real `prefsPath()` is `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/meshcraft/prefs.ini` (or `~/.config/meshcraft/prefs.ini`), matching this row's own verification command exactly; `PrefsAlg` (its persisted-fields struct) has no `apiKey` member. |
| STAB-0622 | ✅ | P0 | Verify API key not logged to stderr/stdout | `src/MeshCraft/AiAssistant.cpp` | `grep -n "std::cout\|std::cerr\|printf\|fprintf" src/MeshCraft/AiAssistant.cpp` → only match is unrelated (`\u%04x` escape formatting in the JSON helper). `apiKeyCopy`'s only use is building the `x-api-key` HTTP header. |
| STAB-0623 | ✅ | P1 | Verify no `std::system()` calls (command injection risk) | all `.cpp` | `grep -rn "std::system\|::system("` across `src/`, `mc3/src`, `mc3togltf/src`, `mc3tomcb/src`, `mcb/src` → 0 hits. |
| STAB-0624 | ✅ | P1 | Verify no `popen` calls | all `.cpp` | Same scope as STAB-0623 → 0 hits. |
| STAB-0625 | ✅ | P1 | Verify include path traversal prevention | `mc3/src/Mc3XmlParser.cpp` | Actually tested (not just read): `<include file="../../../../../../../../etc/passwd"/>` → `Mc3Document::loadFromFile` throws a clean `"Failed to load <include> file '...': Error=XML_ERROR_PARSING_TEXT..."` (the traversed path isn't valid XML, so `tinyxml2` rejects it) — no crash, no hang, no file-content leak into the scene. Residual: the thrown message does echo the resolved absolute path, a minor file-existence probing signal; low severity given the threat model (a local desktop editor run by a user who already has filesystem access as themselves, not a server processing untrusted uploads). |
| STAB-0626 | ✅ | P1 | Define include allowed-root policy | `mc3/src/Mc3XmlParser.cpp` | Already satisfied by this session's `MC3_FORMAT.md` "Include" section (STAB-0594): "`file` is resolved relative to the file that contains the `<include>` element, not relative to the top-level scene file" — states the exact policy asked for. |
| STAB-0627 | ✅ | P1 | Verify registry DB path cannot escape to system paths | `src/MeshCraft/ModelRegistry.cpp` | Both real call sites of `registry_.open(...)` (`MeshCraftApplication_UiAi.cpp:284`, `MeshCraftApplication_UiRegistry.cpp:19`) use `ModelRegistry::defaultPath()` exclusively — a fixed `$HOME/.meshcraft/modelregistry.sqlite3` (or `.` fallback). No UI text input exists anywhere that lets a user (or AI response) supply an arbitrary registry DB path — there's no injection vector because there's no user-facing path parameter for this at all. |
| STAB-0628 | ✅ | P1 | Verify malformed XML does not cause infinite loop | `mc3/src/Mc3XmlParser.cpp` | Actually tested: a 10MB file of repeated `<a` (5,000,000×) → `Mc3Document::loadFromFile` throws `XML_ERROR_PARSING_ELEMENT` in **~7ms**, not the 5s budget the verification text allows. |
| STAB-0629 | ✅ | P1 | Verify huge texture URI does not overflow buffer | `mc3/src/Mc3XmlParser.cpp` | Actually tested: a 10MB-length `<texture uri="...">` value → parses cleanly in ~53ms, `tex.uri.size() == 10000000` (exact, no truncation) — `attr()`/`std::string` have no fixed-size buffer to overflow; this only *could* be a risk in code using a fixed `char[]`, which texture URI parsing doesn't. |
| STAB-0630 | ✅ | P2 | Verify untrusted OBJ file does not crash mesh builder | `mc3togltf/src/MeshBuilder.cpp` | Tested negative out-of-range indices (tinyobjloader rejects cleanly) and literal `nan` (parsed as 0.0, harmless); found + fixed a real gap: infinite coords (e.g. `1e400`) parsed successfully and silently produced spec-invalid glTF (`inf` floats, `null` in accessor min/max) — now rejected with a clean error via the same catch-and-skip path; added permanent `mc3togltf_obj_robustness` ctest |
| STAB-0631 | ✅ | P2 | Verify network timeout prevents indefinite hang | `src/MeshCraft/AiAssistant.cpp` | Same fix/test as STAB-0383 — a mock server that hangs indefinitely is bounded by the (now overridable) read timeout instead of hanging `sendAsync()` forever |
| STAB-0632 | ✅ | P2 | Verify no secrets in autosave or temp files | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication_FileOps.cpp` | `grep -n "apiKey\|ANTHROPIC"` in `MeshCraftApplication_FileOps.cpp` (autosave/backup) → 0 hits; autosave/backup serialize `Mc3Document` only (the scene), which has no API-key field anywhere in its type hierarchy. |
| STAB-0633 | ✅ | P2 | Verify AI apply requires undo-capable state | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication_UiAi.cpp` | Confirmed: `document_` is assigned from `aiPendingDoc_` at exactly one call site (`MeshCraftApplication_UiAi.cpp:266`, the "Apply to Scene" button), unconditionally preceded on the immediately prior line by `pushUndo()` (which deep-copies the pre-mutation `document_` onto `undoStack_` — `MeshCraftApplication_Commands.cpp:323-329`); no other code path reaches that assignment, so apply-without-undo-push is impossible by construction, not just convention |
| STAB-0634 | ✅ | P2 | Verify SQLite parameterized queries prevent SQL injection | `src/MeshCraft/ModelRegistry.cpp` | Every query (`search`/`save`/`remove`) uses `sqlite3_prepare_v2` + `sqlite3_bind_text`/`sqlite3_bind_int64` for every field (query, group, name, variant, xml, tags, description, source, id) — no string concatenation of user input into SQL text anywhere. The one string concatenation present (`"%" + query + "%"` for the `LIKE` wildcard) builds a *bound parameter's value*, not SQL text, so it's safe (note: `%`/`_` in `query` itself act as LIKE wildcards, a minor UX quirk not a security issue — flagged but not acted on earlier this session too). |
| STAB-0635 | ✅ | P3 | Verify tmp file race condition: unique tmp path per process/thread | `src/MeshCraft/ModelRegistry.cpp`, `src/MeshCraft/AiResponseAlgorithms.hpp`, `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication_UiAi.cpp` | **Found a real gap** (the Key File column's `AiAssistant.cpp` was a slight misattribution — the actual 3 tmp-file call sites live in the files listed): each used a plain per-process `std::atomic<int>` counter starting at 0 on every launch, with no PID or random component — two MeshCraft processes (or an app instance + a test run) sharing the same OS temp directory could collide on the exact same filename. Fixed via STAB-0611's new shared `uniqueTempPath()` helper (random 64-bit suffix) used at all 3 sites. |

## Architecture Reference (preserved from original plan.md)

| Module | Location | Role |
|--------|----------|------|
| `MeshCraftApplication` | `src/MeshCraft/MeshCraftApplication.cpp` | Game loop, input, scene state, UI draw |
| `SceneRenderer` | `src/MeshCraft/Renderer/SceneRenderer*.cpp` | Renders MC3 scene + gizmos (4 files) |
| `EditorCamera` | `include/MeshCraft/Editor/EditorCamera.hpp` | Orbit/pan/zoom/focus |
| `Mc3Document` | `mc3/` sublibrary | Pure C++ scene data; load/save XML |
| `Mcb` | `mcb/` sublibrary | Binary serialization of Mc3Document |
| `mc3togltf_lib` | `mc3togltf/src/` | glTF/GLB export (GltfExporter + MeshBuilder + CsgEvaluator) |
| `ModelRegistry` | `src/MeshCraft/ModelRegistry.cpp` | SQLite asset store |
| `AiAssistant` | `src/MeshCraft/AiAssistant.cpp` | Claude API integration |
| CNA | `../cna` | SDL3 window, GL context, input, audio — DO NOT MODIFY |

### Hard Constraints
- **No CNA source changes** without owner permission.
- **No `${meta-gl_SOURCE_DIR}/include`** in CMakeLists.txt.
- **No `Mc3Document` public API changes** without checking `mc3togltf` and all test XMLs.
- **`file(GLOB_RECURSE)`** — new `.cpp` files require cmake reconfigure.
- **No new features** until gates S0–S6 are green.
